PRACTICE TEST 1: E-COMMERCE & SMART LOGISTICS
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update|the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)

Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second

and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)

95c8 AS 100% -  Normal text  Arial  | - 11] +  BIUA  EX  Editing
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)

Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.

using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staffare
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.

buser moe, where customers cal schedule recurng weekly evenes; 7.,,?.
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE <<include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

PRACTICE TEST 1:
1. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and prøcess payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
ENG  2:28 PM
US  4/21/2026

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.

Q s  Normal text -  Arial  I - 11  +  BIUAGE
2
Đề ôn tập
PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
^  ENG  2:30 PM
US  - E 4/21/2026

reature is tie  buscion moe, where customers can schecue recunny weekly unvenes, T.,, ?ee
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE <<include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
ENG  2:33 PM
US  4/21/2026

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
ENG  2:37 PM
US  4/21/2026

Đề ôn tập
PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
^  ENG  2:38 PM
US  4/21/2026

Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE ‹include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventbry management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)

PRACTICE TEST 1:
I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment falls, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)

PRACTICE TEST 1:

I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)

backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
5 of 11
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE ‹include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

I. CASE STUDY
"SwiftMart" is a rapidly growing online grocery platform currently struggling with a manual
backend that leads to frequent delivery delays. The company has commissioned an "Integrated
Fulfillment System" (IFS) to digitize its operations. Customers use the web and mobile
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
All idontific

US  4/21/2026

3  6
platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
must maintain 99.9% uptime, as any downtime directly results in lost revenue.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE <<include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

PRACTICE TEST 3: SMART GYM & FACILITY

platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE <<include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

platform to browse products, manage their shopping carts, and process payments. A unique
feature is the "Subscription Mode," where customers can schedule recurring weekly deliveries;
the system automatically generates these orders every Monday. Delivery Riders use a
dedicated mobile interface to accept delivery assignments, navigate to customer locations
using integrated maps, and update the order status to "Delivered." Warehouse Staff are
responsible for inventory management, including updating stock levels and marking items as
"Damaged" or "Out of Stock." When inventory for a high-demand item falls below a certain
threshold, the system automatically triggers a "Restock Alert" to the Procurement Team.
Managers oversee the platform by viewing real-time sales analytics, managing promotional
discount codes, and handling customer refund requests for missing items.
Technically, the IFS must integrate with a third-party Payment Gateway and a Mapping
Service API. The system must handle up to 5,000 transactions per hour with a sub-2-second
response time for checkout. Security is paramount; all payment data must be PCI-DSS
compliant. If a payment fails, the system offers an optional "Retry Payment" path. The system
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)
1. Identify ALL actors in the IFS. For each actor, state their primary goal and whether they
are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)
2. List at least EIGHT use cases for the IFS. For each use case, state the initiating actors)
and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)
3. Draw a complete UML Use Case Diagram for the IFS. The diagram must include: (0.8
points)
• All identified actors and major use cases.
• At least ONE <<include>> and ONE <<extend>> relationship with rationales.
• Any actor-generalisation relationships that apply.

"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
